People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah… sometimes. I try not to do it, because I know it can be hard for people to follow me.

Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try to keep it clear, I really do.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… well, I guess it was something that happened at work. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but I got kind of worked up in a staff meeting and started, like… acting out a fall-prevention drill with the chairs. I was waving my arms around and showing what I meant, and I guess I couldn’t really stop even when people were telling me to sit down.\n\nI know that probably wasn’t the right way to handle it, so… I’m here because they thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your behavior in the staff meeting was unusual or out of the ordinary for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it was out of the ordinary, if I’m being honest. I don’t usually, you know, get up in a meeting and start moving chairs around or acting things out like that.\n\nI was just trying to explain the fall-prevention thing, but I got really worked up and started waving my arms and demonstrating it, and then… I couldn’t seem to stop, even when people were telling me to sit down. I know that probably looked strange.\n\nDoctor: Did you believe that your actions in the meeting were based on something real that others might not have understood?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess part of me did. I know it might sound like I’m making excuses, and I’m not trying to be difficult or anything.\n\nIt’s just… even when I’m calm, I still kind of feel sure my supervisor and a couple people there are watching me or keeping notes on me. So in the meeting, I think I felt like I had to prove I did understand safety stuff. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your supervisor and those people are watching you or keeping notes on you now, outside of the meeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people. But yes, I still kind of feel that way sometimes, even when I’m not upset.\n\nLike my supervisor and a couple people at work… I get this feeling they’re keeping notes on me, or making little comments to see how I’ll react. It feels like they’re testing whether I’m safe to be around the seniors. I know maybe that sounds like a lot, but it does feel real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisor and those people are actually watching you or keeping notes on you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say it’s just a feeling, because I know that sounds better. But honestly, it feels like it’s actually happening.\n\nEven when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and a couple people at work are keeping notes on me, or making little comments to test me. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe to be around the seniors. I know that may sound like I’m overreacting, but it doesn’t feel imaginary to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a straightforward and organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I try to answer straight, I really do. I don’t want to sound scattered.\n\nIt’s like my mind jumps too fast—like I’ll start thinking about work, then church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop or something—and by the time I try to say the first thing, I kind of lose it. Then I worry I’m not explaining it right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas like that all the time, or is it just when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: It’s… not all the time, I don’t think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it does happen a lot more when I’m stressed or anxious, yeah. My mind will jump from my job to church to my wife to some new workshop idea so fast that I lose the first thought before I can say it right. Then I’m trying to catch up with myself, and I know it probably comes out messy.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: When I’m calm… it’s less, I think. I don’t want to say it never happens, because it does sometimes.\n\nLike even if I’m just sitting there, my mind can still go from my job to church to my wife, then to some new workshop idea, and it’s so quick I lose what I was trying to say first. But it’s worse when I’m stressed. When I’m relaxed, I can usually manage it better… I think. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sticking to one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do sometimes. I try not to, because I know it can be frustrating for people.\n\nI’ll start talking about one thing, like my job, and then my mind jumps to church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop, and it happens so fast that I kind of lose the first thing I was trying to say. Then I’m like… trying to get back to it, but I don’t always say it right.\n\nI don’t mean to go off-topic. I’m trying to stick with it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you don't quite fit in with the people around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming other people or anything.\n\nLately I guess I don’t really reach out much. My wife says I’ll just sit in the living room staring at the TV with the sound real low, and I don’t call people from church like I used to. Even little chores, simple stuff I know needs doing, I just… don’t get to it.\n\nSo maybe that makes me feel like I’m kind of off to the side from everybody. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your supervisor and those people are watching you or keeping notes on you even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know how that sounds, and I’m not trying to make trouble for anybody.\n\nBut even when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and maybe a couple people there are keeping notes on me. So in the meeting, I think I felt like I had to prove I did understand safety stuff. I know maybe that sounds like a lot, but it does feel real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisor and those people are actually watching you or keeping notes on you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say it’s just a feeling, because I know that sounds better. But honestly, it feels like it’s actually happening.\n\nEven when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and a couple people at work are keeping notes on me, or making little comments to test me. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe to be around the seniors. I know that may sound like I’m overreacting, but it doesn’t feel imaginary to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a straightforward and organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I try to answer straight, I really do. I don’t want to sound scattered.\n\nIt’s like my mind jumps too fast—like I’ll start thinking about work, then church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop or something—and by the time I try to say the first thing, I kind of lose it. Then I worry I’m not explaining it right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas like that all the time, or is it just when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: It’s… not all the time, I don’t think. My mind will jump from my job to church to my wife to some new workshop idea so fast that I lose the first thought before I can say it right. I know it might sound like I’m making excuses, and I’m not trying to be difficult or anything.\n\nIt’s just… even when I’m calm, I still kind of feel sure my supervisor and a couple people there are watching me or keeping notes on me. Like they’ll make these little comments that feel like tests, like they’re trying to show I’m not safe to be around the seniors. So in the meeting, I think I felt like I had to prove I did understand safety stuff. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your supervisor and those people are watching you or keeping notes on you now, outside of the meeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m blaming people. But yes, I still kind of feel that way sometimes, even when I’m not upset.\n\nLike my supervisor and a couple people at work… I get this feeling they’re keeping notes on me, or making little comments to see how I’ll react. It feels like they’re testing whether I’m safe to be around the seniors. I know maybe that sounds like a lot, but it does feel real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your supervisor and those people are actually watching you or keeping notes on you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say it’s just a feeling, because I know that sounds better. But honestly, it feels like it’s actually happening.\n\nEven when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and a couple people at work are keeping notes on me, or making little comments to test me. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe to be around the seniors. I know that may sound like I’m overreacting, but it doesn’t feel imaginary to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a straightforward and organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I try to answer straight, I really do. I don’t want to sound scattered.\n\nIt’s like my mind jumps too fast—like I’ll start thinking about work, then church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop or something—and by the time I try to say the first thing, I kind of lose it. Then I worry I’m not explaining it right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas like that all the time, or is it just when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: It’s… not all the time, I don’t think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it does happen a lot more when I’m stressed or anxious, yeah. My mind will jump from my job to church to my wife to some new workshop idea so fast that I lose the first thought before I can say it right. Then I’m trying to catch up with myself, and I know it probably comes out messy.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: When I’m calm… it’s less, I think. I don’t want to say it never happens, because it does sometimes.\n\nLike even if I’m just sitting there, my mind can still go from my job to church to my wife, then to some new workshop idea, and it’s so quick I lose what I was trying to say first. But it’s worse when I’m stressed. When I’m relaxed, I can usually manage it better… I think. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sticking to one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do sometimes. I try not to, because I know it can be frustrating for people.\n\nI’ll start talking about one thing, like my job, and then my mind jumps to church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop, and it happens so fast that I kind of lose the first thing I was trying to say. Then I’m like… trying to get back to it, but I don’t always say it right.\n\nI don’t mean to go off-topic. I’m trying to stick with it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you don't quite fit in with the people around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming other people or anything.\n\nLately I guess I don’t really reach out much. My wife says I’ll just sit in the living room staring at the TV with the sound real low, and I don’t call people from church like I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your supervisor and those people are watching you or keeping notes on you even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know how that sounds, and I’m not trying to make trouble for anybody.\n\nBut even when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and maybe a couple people there are keeping notes on me. Not just at work exactly… like the little comments they make, or things that get repeated, it feels like they’re testing me. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe to be around the seniors.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m reading too much into it, but it still feels real to me. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that people are watching you or plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess outside of work too, in a way. I don’t want to sound paranoid or make it bigger than it is.\n\nIt’s mostly my supervisor and maybe a couple people from work. Even when I’m calm, I still feel like they’re keeping notes on me somehow, or watching how I act, and making little comments to test me. Like they’re trying to build a case that I’m not safe to be around the seniors.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer you honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble finishing your thoughts when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I try not to do it, because I know it can be hard for people to follow me.\n\nIt’s like my mind jumps around too fast — from my job, to church, to something my wife said, then maybe some idea for a workshop or project — and by the time I’m trying to explain the first thing, I’ve kind of lost it. So I’ll stop or start over.\n\nI don’t mean to ramble. I’m trying to answer straight, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express yourself clearly when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try to keep it clear, I really do.\n\nIt’s just my thoughts move fast, like I’ll start talking about work, and then my mind jumps to church, or my wife, or some idea I had for a workshop, and then I can’t get back to the first thing the right way. So it comes out kind of mixed up.\n\nI’m not trying to confuse anybody. I just lose the thought before I can say it right sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find your thoughts are still disorganized and hard to express when you're not feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not as bad maybe, but it can still happen even when I’m not upset.\n\nMy mind just moves too fast sometimes. I’ll be trying to say one thing, like about my job, and then it jumps to church, or my wife, or some idea I had for a workshop, and then I kind of lose the first thought before I can say it right.\n\nI don’t know if that’s what you mean by disorganized, but… yeah, it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally trust other people?\n\nPatient: I try to, yeah. I don’t want to be the kind of person who thinks bad about everybody.\n\nBut… there are certain people I don’t fully trust. Like at work, my supervisor and maybe a couple others — even when I’m calm, I still feel like they’re keeping notes on me or watching how I act. Sometimes they’ll make little comments, and it feels like they’re testing me, like trying to prove I’m not safe around the seniors.\n\nI know that probably sounds… I don’t know, maybe too much. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d like to think most people are trying to help, or at least not hurt me. I don’t want to go around assuming the worst.

But… with some people at work, it doesn’t feel that way. Like my supervisor and a couple others — even when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure they’re keeping notes on me, watching me, making little comments to test me. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe around the seniors.

I know that might sound strong, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.

Then I’m trying to catch up with myself, and I know it probably comes out messy.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your mind jumping between ideas when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: When I’m calm… it’s less, I think. I don’t want to say it never happens, because it does sometimes.\n\nLike even if I’m just sitting there, my mind can still go from my job to church to my wife, then to some new workshop idea, and it’s so quick I lose what I was trying to say first. I try not to, because I know it can be frustrating for people.\n\nI’ll start talking about one thing, like my job, and then my mind jumps to church, then my wife, then some idea for a workshop, and it happens so fast that I kind of lose the first thing I was trying to say. Then I’m like… trying to get back to it, but I don’t always say it right.\n\nI don’t mean to go off-topic. I’m trying to stick with it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you don't quite fit in with the people around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. My wife says I’ll just sit in the living room staring at the TV with the sound real low, and I don’t call people from church like I used to. Even little chores, simple stuff I know needs doing, I just… don’t get to it.\n\nSo maybe that makes me feel like I’m kind of off to the side from everybody. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your supervisor and those people are watching you or keeping notes on you even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know how that sounds, and I’m not trying to make trouble for anybody.\n\nBut even when I’m calm, I still feel pretty sure my supervisor and maybe a couple people there are keeping notes on me. Not just at work exactly… like the little comments they make, or things that get repeated, it feels like they’re testing me. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that people are watching you or plotting against you outside of work?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess outside of work too, in a way. So I’ll stop or start over.\n\nI don’t mean to ramble. Like they’re trying to prove I’m not safe around the seniors.\n\nI know that might sound strong, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that people are watching you or plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to say everybody is plotting against me or anything like that.\n\nBut with my supervisor, and maybe a couple people at work, I still feel it.
